ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00097_d7e3900	Mr President. Mr. Minister of Finance! Mr. Minister of Economic Affairs! Ladies and gentlemen! With its integration package, the Federal Government has presented a concept for the efficient reorganisation of immigration policy and for a better orientation of the labour market in Austria. It has presented one of its central constituencies, one of its central points in recent years. Three key points have actually been reached under the title "Integration before new immigration": For the first time – in a first step – we have reduced the quota for immigration. Only 2,400 key workers can come to Austria, i.e. those who need Austria for an efficient labour market. For the first time in history, we have a government that adopts an integration law – this is also an important point. And we have essential measures to combat social abuse – on the one hand, through the integration treaty, on the other hand, through the law to improve the fight against undeclared work and increase penalties. These are the three main points of a responsible labour market and immigration policy in this country, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have now taken care of what has been called for over the past few years. In full-page advertisements, for example, in 1992 – Mr ÖGB President Verzehnitsch will now listen very attentively to me because he has turned this listing –: "For order on the labour market!", the Trade Union Confederation, together with the Minister of Social Affairs, has given its full support in 1992. And there it says: "The opening of the borders of Eastern Europe has brought Austria opportunities, but also problems. More and more Eastern Europeans want to go the way in the ‘golden west’. Austria must respond because it cannot be an immigration country". Yes, Mr Vergestnich, we have implemented this. Austria is not an immigration country, and it will stay there! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] For the first time, however, we also – and this is very important – have created the definition of "key labour force", because so far, under this title, cheap workers have always come to Austria and thus started a competition for displacement. It's impossible this time. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Silhavy.]] "key labour force" is defined by the high qualification and for the first time also by income. 1 962 € as a minimum limit of income, these are qualified professions, these are those scientists, technicians, managers we need in our economy that we get and we choose when they can come to us. This is also true in the sense of responsible governance, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. That is, the AMS, the labour market service provided by the Red Chamber of Workers and therefore fully under the control of the Red Chamber of Workers, must first see if it finds an Austrian for every division, for every profession that is asked for, and only then, if no Austrian could be found, does the replacement force rule enter into force, then a seasonal rite can be brought. And this is the right procedure for the economy and for the labour market, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have set health checks for the immigrants. If anyone wants to settle with us, he'll have to bring a health certificate. This is now a European standard – in Belgium, France, Greece, Great Britain, Luxembourg, Portugal and Spain. I think that is all right, that is a right way and a proper proposal from the government. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have taken measures to combat shone and sham adoptions. ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00097_d7e4094	Mr President. Valuable colleague of government! Ladies and gentlemen! I consider the subject of this current hour, namely "order on the labour market", to be something very important, because this is basically mediated: The labour market is important to us, for us employment has the highest priority in our country! We know that probably 99 percent of the population would sign that, in addition to basic values such as health and family, a job at which you go with joy and with which you can identify, a job that means life assurance for each individual is very important. From the very beginning, therefore, our policy was aimed at a major goal: Full employment in Austria. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Edellinger.]] This is a goal on which we are working with all the result and which we will also achieve. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] At the Barcelona European Council, where we were with the Federal Chancellor and the Foreign Minister, all 15 Member States said it was a question of giving a clear signal that Europe wanted to become the most competitive economic area in the world by the year 2010. – The labour market and employment have thus played a very, very important role in the discussions there. It is about increasing the employment rate. It is about bringing more people into employment. It is about giving women, older workers and young people a perspective. That is why we have used all the instruments in our power, whether it is active labour market policy, whether it is priorities in the field of research and development, education or training – that is, investing in people, that must be the goal – to focus on infrastructure that is simply aimed at giving more people employment in Austria. I think we can be proud that in 2001, some of us have said it was a bad year, on average, 3 148 000 people in Austria have found employment. This is a record in the history of the Second Republic. Never before in Austria had more people employment than in 2001. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have to say at the same time to the area of unemployment, of course, that every single unemployed person is a unemployed person too much. But here too, the situation is that we are behind the Netherlands – and in fact we cannot compare ourselves with the small Luxembourg – the second or third best country in the European Union, with a value of 3.7 percent in February. We can therefore also be proud of what has been achieved in this area. Ladies and gentlemen! Therefore, the labour market, employment are at the centre of our economic policy, financial policy and social policy. We know that future policy is. This is about security of living, income and prosperity for our people. Because this issue is so important to us, we try to think every day about how we can get better. Two very important prerequisites for this are those that are being dealt with today in the current hour, namely the integration treaty and the active fight against abuse. Actually, I consider it a matter of course that Austrian politicians say, "Austria first!" must apply to the labour market and thus the objective of giving jobs to all those living in Austria and ensuring full employment. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The motto must, of course, be to put integration ahead of immigration. It is here that I consider the integration agreement to be a major step towards facilitating integration into our society, culture and the working world. This begins with the language, deals with the topic of key workers and the link between branch and employment law, so that all who are sustainable in Austria also have free access to the labour market, and to forms of social abuse that we need to combat. We therefore also have on today's agenda a point concerning the novel of the Foreign Employment Act. We are dealing with the questions: How can we combat distortions in the labour market? How can we better control the illegal employment of foreigners? How can we combat all the phenomena of undeclared work and economic fraud? I believe that this fight against abuse is in the interests of employees and entrepreneurs in Austria, because it is not acceptable for employees in Austria to be economically abused, exploited and unregistered, not to have insurance protection, to be sent from an insolvenent company to the next and to pay their wages. It is equally unacceptable that there are companies that are without commercial authorisation, without additional wage calculation, pay no VAT and, on the other hand, all honest entrepreneurs who comply with the rules of the game, which do their taxes and taxes on the spot, which have nothing to do with fair competition. We need to bring about a clear fight against abuse here. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! This comprehensive fight against abuse and fraud is therefore a real personal concern for all of us. We want to set new priorities in the area of illegal employment, which focuses on undeclared work, because it is always about the withdrawal of income and turnover taxes and the absence of social security contributions. It is about unauthorised leave of labour, it is about carrying out activities without a commercial right. It is a huge progress if we manage to triple the control bodies in this area. We want to give a very clear political message: Economic abuse of workers, pollution competition in the sense of business abuse on the business side and fraud against taxpayers must not be cavalier offences! We need to increase the penalties together, here we need to achieve effective control prevention. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I would like to thank the clubs and Martin Bartenstein for having succeeded in finding consensus to bring together control of this subject in the financial sector after a long discussion. It was about how we can make the greatest possible synergies, how we can increase efficiency in this fight against abuse. We are trying to put the tax and customs administration together in our own place of business, because we have employees there who have great experience in discovering and combating tax and social tax fraud. I just remember the anti-fraud report sent to the House. Last year we have confiscated 74 million pieces of cigarettes and 1,500 kilograms of drugs to be smuggled by Austria. We helped to expose the flesh scandal. I think that the Früchter scandal has shown us all more than clearly that it is about moving forward this important joint initiative. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Edler.]] On this occasion, I would like to thank the executive bodies, whether they are police, gendarmerie or customs guards, to the financial and customs administration, for their commitment, which is really important for our country, for employment and for employees. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Our focus is on combating organised economic crime. This goes far beyond fiscal and contribution intervention. This has a national economic and social dimension. We feel committed to the rights of employees, we want to relieve the "honestly economy" of controls and ensure fair competition. Therefore, the fight against abuse and the integration treaty are important instruments to ensure our goal of full employment in Austria. Thank you.

In the case of work intermediaries, the prohibition on the simultaneous exercise of work placement and on the transfer of workers is removed. Officecratic barriers, as they used to apply to private and non-profit workers as well as to interest groups and collective professional associations, are now being reduced. These are upgraded, but the labour market service is not weakened but gets competitive. The labour market service will have fewer costs and will also be able to ensure that the 8,000 seasonal eggs that come to Austria are also communicated and properly assigned. This will also be carefully reviewed so that there is no new recovery, while others in Austria who can do this work are unemployed. The amendments to the Foreign Law and the Federal Finance Act have created measures and conditions for the control of illegal employment, which I consider extremely important, since illegal employment in Austria is at risk of the interests of the labour market as well as of the economy. These interests are safeguarded by the measures set out in these legislative amendments. An efficient fight against illegal foreign employment is in the interest of fair competition in Austria and ensures fair pay and proper working conditions. The transfer of control to the customs authorities and the extension of control staff by double to 98 persons will increase control density, control efficiency and improved detection of illegal employment. Disadvantages for workers or deterioration of their standards are not visible to me, and in this respect all claims are lacking in any basis. I can't tell anyone can't, but we don't really need that. We do not need an uncertainty policy either. We Greens also have a lot of positive effects in this area. What we want: uniform nationally, good framework conditions for eco-power; we have also put forward some proposals. ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00097_d7e19758	Ladies and gentlemen! My predecessor, Mr Moser, said it clearly, and I can only agree with it: In Austria, there is a unanimous view that competition policy is not the best. I think we can say that competition policy is part of the economic policy in Austria, but it is hardly traditional in us. I would even say that the significant feature of Austrian competition policy is – and that to this day! – lack of awareness of the problem. In doing so, a functioning competition – the emphasis is really on the word functioning – is certainly the basis for a dynamic market economy. The problem, however, is that, unfortunately, markets usually only work in textbooks by themselves. In reality, in real life, markets are usually characterised by a variety of different market failures. Unfortunately, in real life it is often enough that managers and entrepreneurs often confuse the freedom of the market with achieving a dominant position with this freedom as quickly as possible. The Microsoft case in particular has made it very clear that market abuse can be very diverse – and that market abuse, in particular, inhibits innovation. Therefore, I really believe that it is one of the most important tasks of a state to define rules of play – and to control compliance with these rules – that rules of play that ensure that the market can actually work. If these rules are neglected, this will ultimately harm the entire economy. My previous speaker also pointed out that the deficits in Austrian competition policy – apart from the lack of awareness of the problem – are enormous, which can be seen, as I believe, in the institutional field in particular. The Austrian cartel court is currently equipped, as far as I know, with three judges who have virtually no personal support in a very complex matter. If you compare with other countries, you can very quickly find that there is virtually no other EU country that would not have such an authority in which not at least 30 to 40 experts are employed. However, there are also a number of other shortcomings in this Austrian system: Although the cartel court may, for example, decide what is missing, it is an independent authority in which, so to speak, the overall responsibility for competition policy is established and which can also be better than the liaison body with the European competition authority. There is also a lack of an efficient response and investigation structure for all antitrust matters. Another criticism: the relatively high transparency of the entire system. I believe that transparency and competition really belong together and that there has been quite a need for action there too. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this reform is in fact a proposal that can be accepted with good conscience. Some of these shortcomings, which I have just mentioned, have been eroded. I also believe that the draft is not, so to speak, the nonplusultra. In particular, the criticism that has been made about this – criticism that concerns this whole very complex system – is well justified and comprehensible. The problem is that by having a cartel dish in Austria, we already have a more complicated system from the outset. However, in Austria, there will be practically three competent bodies operating in competition policy in the future. Considering that we now also have a number of different regulatory authorities, which in the broadest sense also have to be taken into account in the field of competition policy, it is seen that the whole system is very vague and complex. This law, as I believe, is a good starting point, but certainly cannot be the ultimate goal of development. For the future, work should be done on the fact that there really is an overall consolidation in the institutional structure in this respect and that there is also a summary of regulatory and competition authorities, which would be a big throw. Hiezu is expected to offer another opportunity in the near future, because it may be necessary, due to changes in EU law, since a new innovation will take place. And in this direction it would be quite useful to work. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I have to say that I very much appreciated the material discussion climate that really existed in this area, and I would also like to thank in particular for the not only very competent, but above all very dedicated work of the responsible officials in the two ministries. [[Abg. Dr. Moser: That's a little exaggeration!]] The restructuring of the fine system – which I think is particularly important – will also help to ensure fair competition efficiently. It is also an essential aspect of the overall project to limit the powers of the official parties – that is, something you have criticized. That's what we do with this. Thus, only the Federal Competition Authority and the Bundeskartelanwalt will have this position in the future, and they are party in those proceedings which have not been initiated on their request. This new competition authority, this independent authority, carries out the tasks of Austria in the context of the implementation of the European antitrust law, is called for intrusion and investigation of relevant facts, is the applicant at the cartel court and is primarily responsible for merger control. In concrete terms, the competition authority must determine primarily against market abuse and restrictions on competition and then submit applications to the Court of First Instance. If these investigations are carried out, then of course this authority has extensive rights, but it is not that it can go beyond our constitutional domestic rights and powers. If the investigative authority is denied documents, it can only proceed by means of a house search order, but does not invade "rambo-like" everywhere. It is true that we in the Committee on Justice, through an amendment, have secured a rule of law procedure for the investigations. And, Mrs Moser, especially for this investigative work, which may have to take decisions on the issue, house search decisions, the involvement of the Court of Justice is justified and very useful, and it makes sense that we have a branch here. That makes it more rule of law! It should not then punish the investigating authority at the same time. This is not our legal tradition, so I very much welcome the current construction. It is not second-class, as you, Mrs Moser, have mentioned, but it is much cleaner and more efficient in the rule of law. I don't see any double-differences you've led. The competences are regulated by clear rules on the rights of participation of the antitrust attorney in merger control, and therefore I do not believe that there is a double track record. In addition, we have already mentioned in the law that cooperation between antitrust attorney and investigation authority or competition authority must work and that mutual administrative assistance is very much guaranteed. Even the inaction of the antitrust court often criticised in the past will no longer take place in the new system. In the future, antitrust attorneys, the abolition of the official parties and the increased use of professional judges will be much more efficient in dealing with abuse, and this will certainly lead to more procedures. The economy that works in a serious way is not afraid of this more process. On the contrary: It knows that fair competition benefits everyone, not just the smaller ones on the market, that antitrust as such contributes to ensuring the quality of the location, that it helps to secure jobs, and that, in particular, economic damage caused by market abuse is averted. I therefore very much welcome this law, and I also welcome the success of finding a consensus with the largest opposition party. Thank you.

It is more important that we work together positively, work together constructively and achieve a common result than talking about how important the social partners are. It was therefore also necessary for the Austrian legislator to create these conditions, even to involve more competition policy in the legislation at all, something that has not yet been done in Austria. The newly created independent competition authority can meet these requirements. In addition to taking up cases, she examines them and explains facts relevant to antitrust law, she is also an applicant for the cartel court, but also carries out the tasks of Austria in the context of the implementation of the European antitrust law. While they continue to have rights of application and the right to comment, they no longer have the importance of doing so in the old system. In any event, it is clear that a competition procedure will not be possible to do so in the future without the expertise of the social partners – and that is a good thing, one wants to ensure a practice-oriented and economic-oriented competition policy. Ladies and gentlemen! A functioning and fair competition is the prerequisite for a dynamic development of an economic location. With the present draft competition law and the amendment of the antitrust law, we create good foundations to strengthen the position of the Austrian economy also in the common market.

This means that many companies that become insolvent do not even have that much to do with the bankruptcy procedure. On the other hand – and this is the pleasure – the KSV report shows that the proportion of rejected bankruptcy applications has decreased significantly, by around 19.3 percent. The answer to this report is: It is the change of criminal provisions on negligent Krida – at least here – so that not every company owner or business owner has to fear to be punished in addition to the blamage of insolvency and the open-minded entrepreneurial failure. I believe that this too should be noted positively: that legal provisions that legislative action can very well have an impact on the behaviour of citizens. A second example: Statistics on private bankruptcy, on the debt regulation procedure for 2001 also show the same trend. The number of bankruptcy applications rejected in the absence of mass decreases. The share of these bankruptcy applications decreased by more than 10 percent. No longer should legal training alone be decisive, but it should also have economic knowledge and knowledge of tax and labour law. Finally, a list of experts should inform the Court of First Instance of who is probably the best administrator for this insolvency. In addition, insolvency abuse should be prevented by certain game rules. It should not be possible that the one who has driven his company to the edge of the bankruptcy will soon set up a reception company and thus be able to pass on the company as a whole to close relatives. This is a kind of preferred "High Noon": As all parties involved have to put the cards on the table, the parties have to present all the evidence, a discussion has to take place, an informed person is to be loaded, and also the judge has to look into the cards and say how he sees the matter provisionally. I am convinced that this type of "high-noon" is a total acceleration, i.e. a concentration of the substance, and I also assume that a majority of comparisons are taking place, because a comparison only has a meaning in practice if one closes it or does not at all, because one usually kills the costs if one finds itself in the sixth negotiation to make a comparison. – It is therefore extremely necessary that this preparatory negotiations be held. It is logical to say that two hours before the closure of the holding, these rulers allowed themselves to be allowed, and then they said: If the operation is closed in the legal second, then the whole thing is no longer worth it, and the creditors should stay on the track. I can say that I have then proposed a bar to the whole process, and the whole story has then come to a different court. But that wasn't an individual case. A mandatory creditor committee must now be set up, which must be convened at the purchase of a business and must also give its consent. The new mode of mass management has already been used. Finally, this will always be a decision of discretion, and that is, I think, well. You can't just let the ball suck like in the lotto, and then the ball falls, and it's the mass administrator XY, who might be totally inappropriate in this case – I don't mean technical – because he doesn't have the organization, the logistics, or because he may not specialize in the industry concerned. It is therefore important that the relevant list of sectors be kept. I am convinced that this will also make a significant improvement in bankruptcy.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00097_d7e22124	Mr President. Dear Minister Böhmdorfer! Ladies and gentlemen! All in all, I am very happy about this great reform work, even if it comes from a wide range of criticisms. However, as the criticism comes from Mrs Bures on the one hand and the real estate agents on the other, it can only be a good compromise, and therefore I am not dissatisfied! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] With this major reform work, we facilitate the acquisition of property in apartments, namely the acquisition of housing property for one or two natural persons. I would like to stress this so that it does not give the impression that only more homosexual communities can now acquire apartments. That's not what it means! Rather, there are two natural persons – of course, that can be mother and daughter, two siblings, friends, communities of life, not only sexes, but communities of every kind. Certainly, the inheritance in which children acquire an apartment that is more common, and it will certainly also occur much more frequently in terms of care, for example by the daughter who, with the mother to be cared for, wants to have a common home, than by an equal sex community. . I would therefore like to mention this because the opposition here is acted as if it had only been innovationized in one direction. We also facilitate the acquisition of ownership at car storage sites, both in a underground and outdoor garage. To create a provision by which property can also be acquired at a car storage site was never and never possible with the Social Democrats. We also facilitate the acquisition of property in warehouses or in substandard apartments. I give you the right that the common "house" will not be of interest. For my incomprehensible reasons, however, it has not been allowed to have a storage space in its possession. And why should it not also be possible for someone to acquire a substandard apartment in ownership, renew it in his sense, rebuild it and invest in it? [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: But this is very cynical!]] We also facilitate the justification of housing property for a single owner on all objects of a house, a so-called temporary property. This creates legal certainty, which is because the future acquirer of an object in the basic book already sees exactly what the property he will acquire. We also facilitate the formation of housing properties throughout the house. In other words, we require that, when it is parified, the whole house is to be divided, because mixed objects were not only very, very difficult to manage, but also in the case of mixed objects, there was legal uncertainty. The new regulation now creates legal certainty. In the preparatory work on this law there was a wealth of wishes. The first wishes were formulated in Laxenburg, and, Mrs Bures, I was also in Laxenburg and heard all of this. There was a wealth of conflicting desires for this novel, and, of course, the position depends on the situation of interest. We therefore had to find a balanced balance between the management, maintenance and efficient care of houses on the one hand and individual property rights on the other. We have been guided by the basic principles that we have always been aware of in order not to lose sight of. First of all, it was about harmonizing the management costs and avoiding further fragmentation of law. Therefore, the maintenance of the new housing property law is as defined as in the rental law. Secondly, under no circumstances did we want to cut down existing rights. We did that too. We have not intervened in existing rights, and therefore I do not understand why social democracy is so critical of this law! The third principle was that we want to get away from the forced management to a free ownership decision. We don't want to make a mistake about the owners of the house in their decisions, and we don't want to hinder them at all. The fourth principle was that we want to make the administration and maintenance of the houses more efficient, especially in the education of villas. We could not take all things into account, especially if they were diametrically contrary to the other principles. For example, the principle of unanimity in the case of extraordinary measures for administrators is often very laborious, I admit. It is difficult to bring about unanimous decisions. But it is not to be seen why the majority of the owners of extraordinary measures and extraordinary investments are to be overrun by the majority. The same applies to the mandatory owner's meeting, which must take place every two years. This is admittedly an effort for the administrators. However, I believe that the owners have a right to meet at least every two years, discuss their property and make decisions. With regard to the introduction of a owner's representative in the event of problems with the administrator, administrators who work seriously may be able to bring dissatisfaction to the house. I hope, however, that this institution will be more supportive for the administrators and that there will be a consensus here. They see that there were conflicting interests in this law reform work. It's a very big reform work. I would like to thank the Minister for his initiative. He said: We write, starting with § 1, a new law that is much easier to read and also easier and more efficient to implement. – I think that has succeeded, Minister!

Point Report of the Transport Committee on the Government Template (873 of the Supplements): Letter of amendment to the Constitution of the International Telecommunications Union (Gen. 1992), as amended by the Conference of Governmental Plenipotentiaries (Kioto 1994) and Appendices; amendments adopted by the Conference of Governmental Plenipotentiaries (Minneapolis 1998) and Appendices, Declarations and Reservations (1057 of the Supplements)

ParlaMint-AT_2002-03-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00097_d7e28612	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The telecommunications industry is constantly changing both from the supplier side and from the manufacturer side as well as from the side of the structure. It is precisely liberalisation in this sector and the provision of private enterprises that the International Telecommunications Union has made it necessary to adapt to these circumstances. This also made it necessary to amend the telecommunications contract. This is particularly the case in three points. Firstly, there has been an opening up of private companies. This is where private companies are to participate in the competition and cooperate with the States. This is especially the case in the recast of the telecommunications contract. This ensures that the exchange of know-how between countries and companies is effective and that 189 Member States benefit from it. [[In the case of the ÖVP and the Freeman and the Abg. Marizzi.]] Secondly, there is a new financial presentation. It is essential here that Member States take account not only of the expenditure side but also of the revenue side in the future. This is very pleasant for the Member States because it ensures that money is spent only if it is available. Austria makes a contribution of about €180 000 for this. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Third, the structure has been reorganised – every two to three years at an international conference – and the membership arrangements have been re-established. Austria has maintained its original reservations. For the future of the whole telecommunications sector, this Treaty is an important instrument. Today's approval of this instrument of amendment ensures that Austria is and remains a full member of the international concert of the Telecommunications Union.
